Like, I know I’m supposed to stay put and actually finish my claim reviews, but I just keep leaving my desk. It's really starting to become a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: When you get the urge to leave your desk, does it feel like something you can't control or resist, or does it feel more like you're choosing to leave even though you know you shouldn't?\n\nPatient: I guess it's more like I'm choosing to leave, even though I know I shouldn't. Like I mentioned, I know I’m supposed to stay put and actually finish my claim reviews. It's not like a physical force is pulling me out of my chair or anything, but... I just keep getting up from my workstation anyway. It's just really hard for me to stay seated and get the work done.\n\nDoctor: When you're sitting at your desk trying to work, are you able to focus on the insurance claims themselves, or do you find your mind wandering and having trouble concentrating on the task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my mind definitely wanders. I usually end up having to reread the same section of a file a few times before it actually registers.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this difficulty concentrating and staying focused? Has this always been something you've struggled with, or is this something newer that started more recently?\n\nPatient: Well, the problems at work specifically... they've been going on for about 15 months now. That's when my department went through a big reorganization. Before that, I don't know, maybe I struggled a little, but it really became an issue after that reshuffle.\n\nDoctor: Before that reorganization 15 months ago, when you were in your old department, did you have similar trouble staying focused and sitting still, or was that really not an issue for you back then?\n\nPatient: Well, looking back... it wasn't exactly brand new for me. I mean, I guess it wasn't as big of a problem at work before the reorganization, but I've always kind of struggled with this stuff. Like, in elementary school, my teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but... I was always losing my worksheets or missing instructions. They were constantly having to remind me to just finish my classwork. So I guess the focus thing has been around for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school and your teachers were reminding you to finish your classwork, were you also the kind of kid who had trouble sitting still? Like, did you fidget a lot or feel restless when you had to stay in your seat?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't remember exactly how much I moved around as a kid, but I definitely do that kind of thing now. Like, if I'm in a training or one of our longer team meetings at work, I'm usually bouncing my leg or clicking my pen. I just end up shifting around in my chair a lot, even when I'm really trying to look focused.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school, did your teachers or parents ever mention that you were hyperactive, restless, or that you had trouble sitting still in class or at home?\n\nPatient: I really don't recall anyone ever saying I was hyperactive back then. It was mostly just the losing things and not finishing my work that they talked about. I don't think sitting still was a major issue for me as a kid, or at least no one ever brought it up to me or my parents that I know of.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you bounce your leg, click your pen, and shift around in your chair during meetings at work now. When did that start happening—was that also something that picked up around the time of the reorganization 15 months ago, or have you been doing those kinds of fidgety movements for most of your life?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, to be honest. I can't pinpoint exactly when it started. I guess it's possible it picked up around the time of the reorganization, or maybe I've been doing it for a while and just didn't really notice until lately. Like I said before, it's mostly in trainings or those longer team meetings. That's when I'm usually bouncing my leg, clicking my pen, or just shifting around in my chair a lot, even when I'm really trying to look like I'm focused on what's going on. I don't really notice myself doing that stuff when I'm paying bills or doing other things at home.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something at home that requires you to really concentrate and stay focused—like reading something important, doing taxes, or working on a hobby project that demands attention—do you find your mind wandering or do you feel the urge to get up and move around, or does your concentration stay pretty steady in those situations?\n\nPatient: Honestly, my concentration isn't great at home either, but I wouldn't say I feel the urge to get up and move around. Before that, I don't know, maybe I struggled a little, but it really became an issue after that reshuffle.\n\nDoctor: Before that reorganization 15 months ago, when you were in your old department, did you have similar trouble staying focused and sitting still, or was that really not an issue for you back then?\n\nPatient: Well, looking back... it wasn't exactly brand new for me. I mean, I guess it wasn't as big of a problem at work before the reorganization, but I've always kind of struggled with this stuff. Like, in elementary school, my teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but... I was always losing my worksheets or missing instructions. They were constantly having to remind me to just finish my classwork. So I guess the focus thing has been around for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school and your teachers were reminding you to finish your classwork, were you also the kind of kid who had trouble sitting still? Like, did you fidget a lot or feel restless when you had to stay in your seat?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't remember exactly how much I moved around as a kid, but I definitely do that kind of thing now. Like, if I'm in a training or one of our longer team meetings at work, I'm usually bouncing my leg or clicking my pen. I just end up shifting around in my chair a lot, even when I'm really trying to look focused.\n\nDoctor: When you were in elementary school, did your teachers or parents ever mention that you were hyperactive, restless, or that you had trouble sitting still in class or at home?\n\nPatient: I really don't recall anyone ever saying I was hyperactive back then. It was mostly just the losing things and not finishing my work that they talked about. I don't think sitting still was a major issue for me as a kid, or at least no one ever brought it up to me or my parents that I know of.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you bounce your leg, click your pen, and shift around in your chair during meetings at work now. I just don't have a clear memory of when that specific stuff began.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at your childhood and teenage years, do you remember being someone who was generally restless or fidgety in situations where you had to sit still—like during car rides, family dinners, or watching TV—even if teachers didn't specifically call it out as a problem?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think so. It seems to mostly just be a work thing.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the fidgeting mostly happens at work.
